**Purpose and scope:** The project's Open Issues Register (Document 10, section 6): the issues of the Phase 1 topics that need Rama's intent or decision, each with the ten fields of Document 10, section 6. It is separate from the foundation's open items (Document 10, section 11). Claude maintains it and ChatGPT reviews it; Rama decides each issue's treatment, or leaves it OPEN (Document 05, section 11.1). It is updated with each topic package and immediately after Rama decides an issue's treatment (Document 05, section 11.1, update timing 6). An unresolved issue stays here, visible, until it is resolved or Rama decides its treatment (Document 05, section 11.3).

## How entries are recorded

- **Issue ID:** `<TOPIC_ID>-NN`. A01's numbers follow the order of the A01 AUDIT's section 10(b); A02's follow the order of the A02 AUDIT's section 12(b); A03's follow the order in which they are raised (batch 1 in the order of the A03 AUDIT's section 19(b)); A04's likewise (batch 1 in the order of the A04 AUDIT's section 17(b)). Each heading keeps the AUDIT's own issue IDs and the STUDY's R numbers, so that every entry can be traced to its AUDIT record.
- **Three kinds of statement are kept apart** in each entry's Resolution field:
  1. FACT / HISTORICAL: what the legacy repository establishes, taken from the AUDIT and its evidence file, and never changed to agree with an answer;
  2. Rama's statement about the legacy system: how it was operated, or what it was meant to do (an intent source under Document 04, section 5.1, with its date);
  3. Rama's treatment for the new system: input to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5). It is not an approved V2 design decision, and no legacy behaviour becomes a V2 requirement automatically.
- **Recommendations** by ChatGPT or Claude are labelled as such and stay PROPOSED until Rama decides them (Document 05, section 3).
- **Status** has two parts. History (what the legacy system was meant to do, or how it was run): ANSWERED BY RAMA, with his statement quoted, or OPEN QUESTION (OPEN FOR RAMA); answers from evidence stay in the AUDIT. Treatment (the new system), with the labels of Document 05, section 3: OPEN QUESTION until Rama gives it; UNDER DISCUSSION while his recorded answer awaits his confirmation of the read-back; AGREED once he has confirmed it. A recording-only entry (A01-25), which records an issue whose treatment Rama left OPEN without a question, shows its history as "not asked". A part of a question that Rama leaves open is shown OPEN. No entry is marked CLOSED without Rama's explicit decision of its treatment (Document 05, sections 8 and 11.3).
- **Where Rama's words are recorded:** the discussion itself is not kept (Document 07, section 3, retention rule), so each entry quotes the words that decide it.
- **Sensitive values:** none. Credentials, tokens and account identifiers are named by type only.

## A01 — System orchestration, startup, runtime and shutdown

**Source:** `A01_AUDIT_2026-11-05.md` (DRAFT, committed at `8ce3215`; passed by ChatGPT's review of 05-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7), section 10(b), with the questions worded for Rama in its section 9, and its evidence file `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A01_SYSTEM_ORCHESTRATION_STARTUP_RUNTIME_AND_SHUTDOWN/`. Section and record references below without a file name are to that AUDIT; line references without a path are to the legacy `main.py` at the baseline `970aabf` (D-21). 25 entries: the 24 of section 10(b), of which 21 carry a STUDY R number (R4 is referenced by two of them, A01-02 and A01-04) and three have none (A01-21, A01-22 and A01-24); and A01-25, a recording-only entry for C32 (no question; added on 06-Nov-2026 with Rama's approval). The ISSUEs of the AUDIT's section 10(a), established by evidence, are not entered here: their treatment for the new system was raised with Rama after these entries, and any that Rama leaves OPEN is added (Document 04, section 5.5). Rama decided their treatment by group on 06-Nov-2026; the decisions are recorded in the Audit Findings Register (`AUDIT_FINDINGS_REGISTER_2026-11-06.md`; Document 10, section 7), first committed with the A01 topic package (Document 05, section 11.1, update timing 5). The A01 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (`A01_RECONCILED_TOPIC_RECORD_2026-11-06.md`, Part D) shows every A01 item that stays OPEN. He left no section 10(a) finding wholly OPEN; the parts he left OPEN are tracked in the entries here: the exact calendar, time-source and time-basis behaviour (A01-09, A01-15 and A01-20; under group D's exception, A01-09 also covers the calendar failure behaviour of C10b, B-33 and C35's malformed special-session entry, and under group F's, A01-15 covers S-42) and the soft-kill and hard-kill details (A01-14). Parts handed to other topics (for example those of M-08, M-11 and M-30) stay with their owners (AUDIT section 8). C32, an ISSUE in neither list, is kept as recorded, with no treatment line (Rama, 06-Nov-2026); it is recorded as A01-25, so that it meets Document 05, section 11.2.

**Discussion:** in batches of four, each read back to Rama for confirmation. Batch 1 (A01-01 to A01-04) was raised on 05-Nov-2026 and answered the same day in a file prepared with ChatGPT; at the read-back, the same day, Rama corrected the retry count (three attempts in total, not four) and confirmed that his answers on exit 5 and Option A are his recollection of the old system's intent; his confirmation raised no other correction. Batch 2 (A01-05 to A01-08) was raised and answered the same day, and Rama confirmed its read-back the same day ("PART A CONFIRMED"). Batch 3 (A01-09, A01-15, A01-18 and A01-20) was raised and answered the same day, and its read-back was confirmed the same day in Rama's reply prepared with ChatGPT ("PART A CONFIRMED, subject to the A01-18 clarification"). Batch 4 (A01-10, A01-11, A01-14 and A01-19) was raised and answered in the same reply, and its read-back was confirmed the same day ("PART A CONFIRMED"). Batch 5 (A01-16, A01-17, A01-21 and A01-22) was raised and answered in that reply; at its read-back Rama stated that he does not remember the legacy intent and accepted his own new-system inputs. Batch 6 (A01-12, A01-13, A01-23 and A01-24) was raised and answered the same day, and Rama confirmed its read-back on 06-Nov-2026. ChatGPT reviewed the first six versions of this register on 05-Nov-2026. All 24 entries have been raised. In the treatment round (06-Nov-2026) Claude's treatment-round file put the eight entries still OPEN for treatment, and the AUDIT's section 10(a) findings in ten groups, to Rama; ChatGPT reviewed the file independently the same day and recommended a choice for each entry and group (its section 8), with the qualification behind each (its sections 3 and 4). Rama adopted those choices, with the full qualifications, as his decisions (his answers: "Adopt section 8" and "Include sections 3-4"). ChatGPT reviewed the recorded decisions the same day and found none recorded wrongly.

### A01-01 — C1 (R1, narrowed; impact question)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A01-01 |
| Topic | A01 |
| Question or discrepancy | After May 2026, was `main.py --interactive` still used, for live trading or for the recovery steps in `docs/disaster_recovery.md`? Its planned use for the first paper days of April 2026 is already answered by the operations documents. |
| Evidence | FACT (C1, KNOWN DEFECT): `main.py --interactive` stops with `NameError` and exit 2 at L2534; the binding broke at commit `ab2c6d2` (05-May-2026). HISTORICAL: the operations documents of 18-Apr-2026 planned the interactive launch for the first paper days, with systemd taking over from the next day (RUNBOOK T5R02; PLAYBOOK T2R08 and T4R01; IS-1.19); `docs/disaster_recovery.md` (2026-08-02) prescribes it for recovery (L113, L134 to L136, L227); the boot record of 26-Aug-2026 shows a headless service start (`docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L115). Section 9, R1. |
| Impact | Bears on the defect's impact only; C1's classification rests on the code and the intent sources and does not depend on the answer (section 9, R1). |
| Owner | Rama (the answer and the treatment); Claude records them (A01). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 1); answered the same day; the read-back confirmed the same day. The treatment was raised in the treatment round (06-Nov-2026) and decided the same day. |
| Next action | ChatGPT reviewed the history record on 05-Nov-2026; ChatGPT reviewed the treatment recorded on 06-Nov-2026 the same day. The treatment is carried to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5). |
| Status | History: ANSWERED BY RAMA (confirmed on 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (06-Nov-2026). |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's statement (05-Nov-2026; confirmed at the read-back the same day): "HEADLESS": the system was operated in headless mode, and interactive mode was not the operating method. The question asked about use after May 2026; the statement is recorded as given, and no date or recovery event is inferred from it. (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED, 06-Nov-2026; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision): in the treatment round of 06-Nov-2026 Rama adopted the choice recommended in ChatGPT's independent review of Claude's treatment-round file (its section 8), with the full qualification behind it (its section 3), as his decision: choice A, accepting Claude's recommendation, in its words: "the new system has ONE start path, headless; no separate interactive start mode. Any manual or recovery start uses the same path and the same checks, so no second, untested path exists." The qualification, in ChatGPT's words: "One headless start path is consistent with Rama's agreed single configured Zerodha-client operating model. Manual and recovery starts should use that same path and safety checks. Do not retain a separate interactive mode. Make clear that an operator can invoke the headless path manually; “headless” must not mean “no operator-controlled recovery”." |

### A01-02 — C2, the L2374 exit (IC-40; with R4)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A01-02 |
| Topic | A01 |
| Question or discrepancy | At a live start, was a missing credential environment variable meant to give exit 5 (docstring, M41C) or exit 6 (code, FIX-163)? |
| Evidence | FACT (C2 part (iii); C12): an unset API-key variable raises `KeyError` at L2361, caught at L2371 to L2374 with a CRITICAL log line and exit 6. The sources conflict (IC-40): exit 5 in the module docstring L17 ("5 invalid args / config / missing env var"), in M41C SU10 (`consolidated_module_41_with_context.txt` L190 to L193: "If missing: print error with env var name, exit 5") and in M41C SU15 (L298); exit 6 in the code, in commit `7fc06ff` (HISTORICAL, 12-Jun-2026, FIX-163: "Falls back to exit code 6 (no restart) on missing token or credential") and in existing record 03 L174. Restart (C4; INTERPRETATION, systemd semantics; whether the declared unit was installed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, B-40): under the declared unit exit 5 is not restarted since 23-Aug-2026 (commit `2f67bb8`), and the token watcher retries it up to 3 times an hour with a fresh token; exit 6 is restarted every 10 s. |
| Impact | Which code was meant decides whether the code's exit 6 departs from intent; with A01-04 it bears on how the service was to react. C2's other parts are classified without it. |
| Owner | Rama; Claude records it (A01). The restart treatment is A01-04's (C4). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 1); answered the same day; the read-back confirmed the same day, with the retry count corrected. |
| Next action | None for the discussion (ChatGPT reviewed this record on 05-Nov-2026). Rama's statement and treatment are carried to the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (Document 07, section 5) and, as Phase 2 input, to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5). |
| Status | History: ANSWERED BY RAMA (his recollection, confirmed on 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (05-Nov-2026). |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence; the conflict between the sources (IC-40) stays recorded, unchanged. (2) Rama's answer (05-Nov-2026): "OPTION B — EXIT 5 (invalid configuration)", which he confirmed the same day as his recollection of what the old system was intended to do ("YES, HIS MEMORY ONLY"). Cross-check: it agrees with the docstring L17 and M41C (SU10, SU15), which do not say whether exit 5 is restarted; FIX-163 chose exit 6 and describes it in its commit as "(no restart)". (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision), in the words of Rama's confirmation: "THREE ATTEMPTS TOTAL. The sequence is: 1. First start attempt. 2. Second attempt (first retry), after 10 seconds. 3. Third attempt (second retry), after a further 10 seconds. If the third attempt also fails, stop retrying and trigger the configured severity warning alerts through Telegram and email." "This means three attempts in total, not three retries. Do not make a fourth attempt." The alert content stands from his first file: "Alerts should explain the failure, identify the failed startup check, state that the retry limit has been exhausted, and confirm that the service has stopped. Never include credential values, tokens, API keys, or other secrets in alerts." The read-back corrected the count: his first file said "FOUR ATTEMPTS IN TOTAL"; his own earlier words were "restart for every 10s for 3 times, then at the end of third time stop and trigger severity warning alerts (Telegram & Email) detailed". In the legacy, the declared unit restarted exit 5 until 23-Aug-2026 and not after it (commit `2f67bb8`), and the token watcher retries it up to 3 times an hour with a fresh token (C4); no legacy source specified this policy. A recommendation on retries is recorded in A01-04. |

### A01-04 — C4 (R4, narrowed)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A01-04 |
| Topic | A01 |
| Question or discrepancy | Besides exit 6, were exit 9 and failures that repeat on every restart meant to be restarted automatically, and was the April start limit meant to remain? |
| Evidence | FACT (C4): the declared unit (`deploy/systemd/trading-system.service`, 2026-08-23) sets `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=10` and `RestartPreventExitStatus=3 4 5`. No explicit start limit is defined in the unit file (no `StartLimitIntervalSec` or `StartLimitBurst`); whether a manager-level override limited restarts is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, as no record of the VM's manager configuration survives; whether the declared unit was installed is also UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (B-40). Documented default (systemd's own documentation, external to the legacy repository): a unit that sets no limit takes the manager's default, 5 starts in 10 s (systemd-system.conf(5): "DefaultStartLimitIntervalSec= defaults to 10s" and "DefaultStartLimitBurst= defaults to 5", the same in systemd 229 and in the current source). INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics): restarts spaced 10 s apart never reach that default; so, if the declared unit was installed and no override applied, exits 1, 2, 6 and 9 would be restarted after 10 s without end, and a failure that repeats on every restart would loop: another program holding the lock port 5001, a database migration refused during market hours, a malformed holiday file or special session, a missing token file, a short `accounts.csv` row. HISTORICAL records of the VM (legacy descriptions, not runtime evidence available to this project): the unit's only recorded drop-in adds `Wants=trading-watchman.service` (Record 03 section 3.2, the testing VM; the read-only inspection of 05-Jul-2026, `docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L169 and L235: "[Unit] + Wants="; still present, not read, on 26-Aug-2026: `docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L363); long restart loops were reported: a "240+ restart crash-loop" on exit 4 on 18-Jun-2026 (commit `ebfe780`) and "infinite holiday alerts on weekends" under `Restart=always` (commit `398dbae`, 05-May-2026); the legacy crash-test notes take the defaults as applying ("5 starts in 10s": `docs/crash_test_info_04jun2026.md` L112, L997 to L998; CT-089 L1191, L1198). Exit 9 is a broker other than Zerodha in `accounts.csv`. The token watcher (`deploy/token_watcher.sh`) caps crash restarts at 3 an hour (L13, L27, L168 to L177) but acts only on a failed or inactive unit, so it never sees the exits that systemd restarts. Exit 6 is answered by evidence: not meant to be restarted (section 9, R4). HISTORICAL: the April start limit (`StartLimitIntervalSec=300`, `StartLimitBurst=3`: STEP3 L104 to L108 and L372 to L375; STEP4A L16; commit `316b335`, 19-Apr-2026; CT-089 relied on it) was removed by commit `ccde039` (22-Apr-2026) together with a change to `Restart=always`, under a comment that the token watcher's gating would stop an always-restart loop from hammering with a stale token; commit `398dbae` (05-May-2026) returned to `Restart=on-failure` and removed that comment, without restoring the limit. For exit 9 and the repeating causes behind exits 1 and 2: no source. |
| Impact | If the declared unit was installed and no manager-level override applied, a repeating failure would restart every 10 s without end, each attempt re-running the early boot steps (C2 and C4; INTERPRETATION); the answer decides treatment, not the classification (section 9, R4). |
| Owner | Rama; Claude records it (A01). The unit file is A17's; the token watcher is A04's file, with its restart contract A13's (cross-referenced). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 1); answered the same day; the read-back confirmed the same day, with the retry count corrected. |
| Next action | None for the discussion (ChatGPT reviewed this record on 05-Nov-2026). Rama's statement and treatment are carried to the RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (Document 07, section 5) and, as Phase 2 input, to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5). |
| Status | History: ANSWERED BY RAMA (his recollection, confirmed on 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (05-Nov-2026). |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence, unchanged; the start-limit wording is the one Rama confirmed on 05-Nov-2026 ("No explicit start limit is defined in the unit file; whether a manager-level override limited restarts is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED"). (2) Rama's answer (05-Nov-2026), Option A: "NO — repeated failures should not restart indefinitely; retain a start limit.", which he confirmed the same day as his recollection of what the old system was intended to do. Cross-check: it agrees with the April start limit, CT-089's expectation and the token watcher's cap of 3 an hour; no surviving source intends unlimited restarts (the comment of 22-Apr-2026 was removed on 05-May-2026). It is not claimed that the legacy configuration had a three-attempt policy. (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision): the same three attempts in total as A01-02 (the first start, then two retries 10 seconds apart; no fourth attempt), then stop and send the severity warning alerts by Telegram and email; no endless restart loop. Rama's words: "Option A with same as C2 (R4): repeat 3 times start > stop & trigger alerts" and, at the read-back, "THREE ATTEMPTS TOTAL" (his first file had said "After four unsuccessful attempts in total, stop"). Recommendation (PROPOSED; not decided by Rama): ChatGPT advised Rama on 05-Nov-2026 to "apply the retry limit consistently to failures classified as retryable. Permanent configuration errors should ideally be distinguished from temporary failures rather than repeatedly retried without purpose." Claude agrees: a missing credential, a broken holiday file or a wrong broker in the account file fails the same way ten seconds later, so retries help only with temporary causes, such as a broker connection that drops for a moment; for A01-02's case the alert, not the retries, brings the fix. The point is for the Phase 2 restart policy. |

### A01-10 — C13 (R12)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A01-10 |
| Topic | A01 |
| Question or discrepancy | Was starting the MIS units and the optional workers before the last guards and the reconcile, and leaving them running after a failed start, deliberate? |

### A01-16 — C31 (R22 part 2)

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A01-16 |
| Topic | A01 |
| Question or discrepancy | Was the lock meant to stop a hand-run copy beside the service despite `PrivateTmp`? |
| Evidence | FACT (C31): the instance lock has two layers: a kernel-owned lock file, `trading-system.lock` in the temporary directory, which the module calls authoritative (X5, `utils/instance_lock.py` L133 to L135), and a bind of the loopback port 5001 (FIX-081), which is fixed: no configuration key exists. The declared unit sets `PrivateTmp=true` (security hardening, STEP3 L115 to L119). INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics): under `PrivateTmp` a copy of `main.py` started by hand locks a different file, so only the port refuses it (consistent with the HISTORICAL 18-Jun-2026 collision at port 5001, `deploy/resume.sh` L4 to L7). The documents assume that the lock file refuses a second copy by itself (`tests/unit/test_instance_lock.py` L1 to L15 and L207 to L214: "before the port is even considered"; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L905 to L907: a standalone `main.py` "is refused by BOTH layers"; `docs/audit/batch3_done_17jul2026.md` L226 to L227); no source mentions the effect of `PrivateTmp` on the lock. HISTORICAL: the April procedure stopped the service before the hand-run first paper day, before any lock existed (RUNBOOK T4R06; PLAYBOOK T3R08; IS-3.15; the lock came with `ab2c6d2`, 05-May-2026). R22 part 1 (a configurable port was intended) is answered by evidence. Section 9, R22. |
| Impact | Treatment; C31 is classified ISSUE without it. |
| Owner | Rama; Claude records it (A01; A17 for the unit). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 5); answered the same day, in Rama's reply prepared with ChatGPT; at the read-back the same day Rama stated the history and accepted his new-system input |
| Next action | None for the discussion (ChatGPT reviewed this record on 05-Nov-2026). The history stays OPEN and is carried forward (Document 05, sections 11.2 and 11.3); the treatment is carried to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5) |
| Status | History: OPEN QUESTION (Rama does not remember; 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (05-Nov-2026, accepted at the read-back) |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's answer at the read-back (05-Nov-2026): "Legacy history: OPEN — Rama does not remember the old system's intended behaviour." (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision), accepted at the read-back of 05-Nov-2026, in the words of Rama's reply: ACCEPTED as a Phase 2 input: "Use a reliable single-instance control." "A second manually started process must not run as a competing live trading process. Detailed mechanism and failure handling are for Phase 2." ChatGPT's recommendation of 05-Nov-2026, put to Rama before the read-back, stays recorded for reference (PROPOSED); the AGREED input is Rama's wording above, and the details are for Phase 2: "The instance lock should prevent a second process from running the trading system regardless of how the first and second processes were launched (systemd service, manual command, recovery command or another supported launcher). Do not rely on a lock file located in a systemd PrivateTmp directory if a manual process can see a different temporary directory. Use one authoritative, atomic lock mechanism in a location shared by all permitted launch contexts, with suitable ownership and permissions. Retain the reserved port as a secondary collision guard, not as the only protection between a service and a manually launched process. If the authoritative lock cannot be acquired or its state cannot be safely established, fail closed: do not proceed to trading activity. Define safe stale-lock recovery so a crashed process does not leave a permanent false lock. This recommendation does not establish what the legacy system was intended to do." |

|---|---|

The session record must show what is actually configured for that trading day, for example INTRADAY, DELIVERY or BOTH. Record the configured mode, not an assumed/fallback value. Exact fields and allowed values are for Phase 2."" ChatGPT's recommendation of 05-Nov-2026, put to Rama before the read-back, stays recorded for reference (PROPOSED); the AGREED input is Rama's wording above, and the details are for Phase 2: "A session record should represent the actual configured account identity and actual trading mode applicable to that session. Do not write misleading fixed placeholders such as "default" or "INTRADAY" when they do not describe the real session. Keep credentials and secrets out of the record. Preserve the original session-start timestamp; later status updates should update separate fields such as last_updated_at/heartbeat_at rather than overwrite the original start time. Where more than one trading mode can apply, record the actual configured mode or define an explicit, unambiguous representation. This recommendation does not decide the legacy intent or assume a particular V2 account/mode architecture." |

### A01-18 — C35 (R11)

|---|---|

The sources conflict: FIX-094 (`core/market_windows.py` L45 to L47, L96 to L97; commit `ee91659`, 17-May-2026: special-session dates are not holidays, "exempts weekend check") and `config/nse_holidays_2026.yaml` L5 to L8 (2026-04-17: Muhurat on 08-Nov-2026, a Sunday; "System treats this as holiday by default. Operator can override if Muhurat session participation is desired"), against SU6 and `utils/holiday_guard.py` L11 (a weekend is never a trading day) and the FIX-189 START cutoff. INTERPRETATION: the operator override that the holiday file mentions does not exist in the code. Section 9, R11. |
| Impact | Treatment; C35 is classified without it. |
| Owner | Rama; Claude records it (A01; A02 for the schema; A12 for the MIS cutoff; A16 and A04 cross-referenced). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 3); answered the same day; the read-back confirmed the same day, with the rule clarified |
| Next action | None for the discussion (ChatGPT reviewed this record on 05-Nov-2026). The history stays OPEN and is carried forward (Document 05, sections 11.2 and 11.3); the treatment is carried to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5) |
| Status | History: OPEN QUESTION (Rama does not remember). Treatment: AGREED (05-Nov-2026, as clarified at the read-back) |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's answer (05-Nov-2026): "LEGACY HISTORY: OPEN." He states that his present preference is not a historical answer, and that no legacy behaviour is to be inferred from it. (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision), as clarified at the read-back of 05-Nov-2026, in the words of Rama's reply: "Do not trade during any Muhurat trading session, irrespective of whether it is scheduled on a weekday, market holiday or weekend. Treat the Muhurat session as a non-trading day for this system. No manual override, special-session setting or calendar exception may enable trading during a Muhurat session." His reason: "Muhurat trading is a shortened session, typically around 1–3 hours, and Rama does not consider it suitable for his trading"; in his own words: "In new system I don't want to trade on muhurt trading sessions, why? Muhurt session will lasts 1-3hours only, So not prefer to trade it in those days be it holiday or weekend". Scope (his answer B to the Budget-day question): "Skip every Muhurat trading session, whatever day of the week it falls on. Do NOT automatically skip a full-length, normal trading session merely because NSE schedules it on a weekend or holiday. A full normal special session, such as the cited Budget-day session, is not excluded by the Muhurat rule. It may be considered for trading subject to normal system controls and the future Phase 2 design." The clarification replaces the first wording of 05-Nov-2026 ("Do not trade Muhurat or other special sessions that fall on NSE holidays. Treat the date as a holiday for this system. Do not use a manual override or automatic special-session configuration to enable trading on that date."): the exclusion is now tied to the session being a Muhurat session, not to the date. Cross-check (external sources, 05-Nov-2026): NSE holds the 2026 Diwali Muhurat session on Sunday 08-Nov-2026 (NSE circular NSE/FAOP/76940 of 19-Oct-2026: normal market 14:20 to 15:40 in the F&O segment, about 80 minutes, within Rama's "1–3 hours"), and published 2026 holiday lists show the day, Diwali Laxmi Pujan, among the holidays that fall on a weekend; the Union Budget session of Sunday 01-Feb-2026 was a full session (normal market 09:15 to 15:30), so it falls outside the rule. |

This ensures that subsequent startup failures—including holiday-file, start-window, lock and configuration failures—are recorded in a durable log. The sources conflict (IC-31): SC7 and SC12 (`module_33_startup_checks.txt`, 2026-04-17) make an outage a warning; P17 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L370 to L376): "CRITICAL on any failure"; SPEC C.1 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L653 to L659): "Telegram CRITICAL + exit(1)"; the rule of 27-Jul-2026 (`main.py` L4022 to L4033), written for the webhook self-check: "DEGRADING IS ONLY SAFE IF SOMETHING ALARMS" (whether it was meant generally is INTERPRETATION). |
| Impact | Treatment; S-44 is classified ISSUE without it. |
| Owner | Rama; Claude records it (A01; A06 for the scanner map; A20 for the pre-flight script). |
| Raised with Rama | 05-Nov-2026 (batch 5); answered the same day, in Rama's reply prepared with ChatGPT; at the read-back the same day Rama stated the history and accepted his new-system input |
| Next action | None for the discussion (ChatGPT reviewed this record on 05-Nov-2026). The history stays OPEN and is carried forward (Document 05, sections 11.2 and 11.3); the treatment is carried to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5) |
| Status | History: OPEN QUESTION (Rama does not remember; 05-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED (05-Nov-2026, accepted at the read-back) |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL: as in Evidence, unchanged; the conflict between the sources (IC-31) stays recorded. (2) Rama's answer at the read-back (05-Nov-2026): "Legacy history: OPEN — Rama does not remember the old system's intended behaviour." (3) Treatment for the new system (AGREED; Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision), accepted at the read-back of 05-Nov-2026, in the words of Rama's reply: ACCEPTED as a Phase 2 input: "Treat a required signal-source outage as a clearly classified dependency failure." Additional Phase 2 discussion point, in Rama's words: "If the required signal source remains unavailable, wait for one hour from the configured trading start time in config.yaml. If it is still unavailable at the end of that hour, automatically stop for the remainder of that trading day and remain stopped until the next day's boot. Example: configured start 10:00; wait until 11:00; if the source is still unavailable at 11:00, stop for the day. This is a Phase 2 input, not a frozen design. Phase 2 must define 'unavailable', recovery detection, late boot handling, alerts and enforcement. Do not settle or implement these details during Phase 1." ChatGPT's recommendation of 05-Nov-2026, put to Rama before the read-back, stays recorded for reference (PROPOSED); the AGREED input is Rama's wording above, and the details are for Phase 2: "A Chartink outage should generate a CRITICAL operator alert and prevent the system from declaring normal trading readiness or accepting new entries while the required scanner feed is unavailable. Prefer starting/keeping the service in an explicit DEGRADED / NOT READY state rather than terminating the whole process immediately, provided the service can still safely monitor and manage existing positions, orders and risk. Retry/recheck availability under a bounded, documented policy; restore trading readiness only after the required scanner checks pass. If safe position/order management cannot be maintained, the system must follow a separately defined protective escalation path. Do not silently continue as fully active with only an unread WARNING. This proposal deliberately distinguishes process availability from permission to initiate new trades; it is not a historical answer and is not a V2 design decision." |

## A02 — Configuration and effective settings

**Source:** `A02_AUDIT_2026-11-08.md` (DRAFT; passed by ChatGPT's complete package review of 08-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7), section 12(b), with the STUDY's questions reviewed in its section 11, and its evidence file `evidence/A02_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-06.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A02_CONFIGURATION_AND_EFFECTIVE_SETTINGS/`. Section and record references below without a file name are to that AUDIT; line references are to the legacy repository at the baseline `970aabf` (D-21). 15 entries. Ten, A02-01 to A02-05 and A02-08 to A02-12, are one for each of the AUDIT's 10 findings that need Rama's intent or decision (section 12(b)). (b) For Rama: was the deployment guide's general TEMP-revert procedure ("Revert TEMP config values ... --apply --confirm" at the switch from Paper to Live) still meant to be followed after the June changes, and, if so, did its scope exclude the daily-loss entry superseded by the 3% authority? (c) `gap_fade_long.min_score`, 0 in the file against 60 in the revert table: ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE (0); not put to Rama. |
| Evidence | (a) FACT (AUDIT 8.3, F-22): `risk.daily_loss_limit_pct` is 0.03 in the file (`config/system_config.yaml` L316); `TEMP_REVERTS` holds 0.05 (`scripts/revert_temp_config.py` L46 to L54). HISTORICAL: 0.05 in the table, the TEMP tracker and the deployment guide (all 2026-06-03); 0.03 set by commit `b27f767` (2026-06-14, "daily_loss_limit_pct 0.05 → 0.03") and declared the sole, permanent daily-loss authority by commit `c69f1fd` (2026-06-24), which implements 12 resolved conflicts "all LOCKED by Rama" and edited the script without changing 0.05, and by the file comment L316; the system map calls the 5% a fixture literal; a test fixture of 2026-07-25 still holds 0.05 as the script's clean state (IC-32). The later-dated sources settle 0.03; the table's 0.05 is a stale entry. HISTORICAL: 60 in the table and the TEMP tracker (2026-06-03); 0 restored by commit `1e41f99` (2026-06-08), whose removed comment said "was 0 -> uses scorer default"; the system map calls the "60" note stale and DEC-04 gives 0 (IC-33). The later-dated sources settle 0; the table's 60 is a stale entry. |
| Impact | (b): following the deployment guide at the baseline would loosen the daily loss limit from 3 % to 5 % of capital at the next restart; the script would not change `gap_fade_long.yaml` (it has no TEMP marker) and would report it as already reverted (F-23). Whether the script was ever run is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (F-25). |

(3) Rama's new-system input (08-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input, confirmed in the read-back): he accepted ChatGPT's suggestion, in his words: "Suggestion accepted, and also ensure that SL % wouldn't exceed 10% (for accidental input) if so immediate warn me before system accepts it, record now deep discuss at new system design" (for (a)); "Suggestion accepted and It must be a broader coverage as much as possible like config.yaml, strategy.yaml, any hardcoded anywhere instead of file/yaml driven?, etc, So record now & deep discuss later while new system design" (for (b)). Recorded as: (a) configuration and risk validation covers the applicable settings of every enabled trading mode, including the delivery counterparts where delivery is enabled; and a proposed guardrail: a stop-loss percentage input above 10% triggers an immediate warning to Rama before the system accepts it, so that an accidental oversized stop-loss is never silently accepted. (b) The configuration audit covers configuration sources as broadly as practicable (`config.yaml`, `strategy.yaml`, other relevant configuration files and the relationships between them), and also looks for trading or configuration values or behaviour hardcoded in source code where approved file or YAML configuration should control them; not every literal in code is an improper hardcode, and Phase 2 distinguishes legitimate implementation constants from values that duplicate or bypass configuration. Consistent with his A02-02 input (no hardcoded settings, no code defaults as a second source, no settings read from `.py` files); the two entries stay separate. It is not an answer to the history question and not a V2 design decision. ChatGPT's suggestion is recorded as restated in ChatGPT's instruction to Claude of 08-Nov-2026. Left for Phase 2 design, not decided: for (a), whether the warning blocks, whether an authorised override is possible, which stop-loss fields, strategies and modes it applies to, and how the percentage is calculated; 10% is his threshold for this warning, not a settled universal risk limit; for (b), the exact audit scope, the source inventory, the detection method, the exclusions and the reporting rules. ## A03 — Database, persistence, migrations and backups

**Source:** `A03_AUDIT_2026-11-10.md` (APPROVED, approved by Rama on 2026-11-11 with the A03 topic package (Decision Register, D-32); passed by ChatGPT's review of 10-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7; corrected at `cfdafdd`, finding M-01 of ChatGPT's review of the issue-discussion plan), sections 18 and 19(b), with its evidence file `evidence/A03_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-10.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A03_DATABASE_PERSISTENCE_MIGRATIONS_AND_BACKUPS/`. Section and record references below without a file name are to that AUDIT; line references are to the legacy repository at the baseline `970aabf` (D-21). 51 entries. A03-01 to A03-08 (batch 1): one for each of the AUDIT's 8 findings whose classification depends on Rama's intent (section 19(b)). A03-09 to A03-27 (batch 2): the database access, transaction and schema questions (STUDY questions 1, 2 other than `--status` and `--dry-run`, 3, 4, 7, 8, 9, 10 part 2, 12 and 14), split where a question holds independent decisions; the AUDIT classifies their findings without Rama's answer. A03-28 to A03-43 (batch 3): the store API and SQL questions (STUDY question 15, part 1, and questions 16 to 22), split in the same way; the AUDIT classifies their findings without Rama's answer. A03-44 to A03-51 (batch 4): the checkpoint, retention, backup and restore-drill questions (STUDY question 6, parts 1 and 3, the rest of question 23, and questions 24 to 28); the AUDIT classifies their findings without Rama's answer. Each entry is one part of a STUDY question, except A03-29 (a part derived from the AUDIT record P-03, the finding of question 15, part 1) and A03-35 (from question 16's cited evidence, P04-S197). A03-08 holds four families, (a) to (d); A03-15 two parts, (a) and (b); A03-33 three parts, (b) to (d); A03-37 two parts, (a) and (b); A03-41 four parts, (a) to (d); A03-46 three parts, (a) to (c); A03-48 three parts, (a) to (c); and A03-49 four parts, (a) to (d); each with its own answer and status. Every STUDY question has now been raised or is covered: question 10, part 1 is answered by evidence, question 2's `--status` and `--dry-run` part is governed by A01-05's agreed treatment, the writer part of question 19 is named by the later-dated source (AUDIT Q-05), and the parts of question 23 on the V3 and evidence JSONL and the undated cron-redirect logs are answered by evidence; none of these is asked. An ID is never reused.

**Discussion:** in batches, each proposed by Claude, reviewed by ChatGPT before Rama sees it, answered by Rama, and read back to him for his confirmation. Batch 1 (A03-01 to A03-08) was put to Rama on 10-Nov-2026 after ChatGPT's fifth review of the plan passed it; Rama answered the same day, through an answers file prepared by ChatGPT at his instruction ("Write all open & your suggestion accepted"): OPEN to every history question (each family of A03-08 separately) and "Accept ChatGPT's suggestion." to every new-system part. History and new-system treatment are recorded separately; an OPEN answer does not reclassify a finding, so the 8 findings stay UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). Batch 2 (A03-09 to A03-27) was put to Rama on 10-Nov-2026 after ChatGPT's review of its proposal passed it (second revision); Rama answered the same day, through an answers file prepared by ChatGPT at his instruction: OPEN to every history question (A03-15 part by part) and "accept ChatGPT's suggestion" to every new-system part except A03-18's, which was not asked (A02 V-11's agreed treatment applies, unchanged). Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed"). ### A03-01 — D-04 (STUDY question 5): Safety settings on connections opened outside the store

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-01 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | Were database connections opened outside `StateStore` (raw `sqlite3.connect` and `db_connect.connect`) meant to use the same safety settings as the store (`foreign_keys = ON`, `synchronous = FULL`)? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies D-04 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 5.3). FACT: the store sets `foreign_keys = ON` and `synchronous = FULL` on every connection; `db_connect.connect` and the raw openers set neither; no raw or `db_connect` writer writes a foreign-key column at the baseline. INTERPRETATION: the library defaults apply on those connections. HISTORICAL: the store docstring and FIX-076 state the settings for the store only; no source states them for raw connections. |
| Impact | Not established. |
| Owner | Rama answers; Claude records. Topic: A03 (`db_connect`); the owners of the raw openers. |
| Raised with Rama | 10-Nov-2026 (batch 1, B1-Q1), after ChatGPT's review of the issue-discussion plan (fifth review, PASS); answered the same day; read back the same day (ChatGPT's review of the read-back: PASS); Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). |
| Next action | None in the intent discussion. D-04 stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED and receives no treatment at A03's treatment round; the new-system input is carried to the Phase 2 proposal (Document 04, section 5.5). |
| Status | History: OPEN QUESTION (Rama answered OPEN on 10-Nov-2026). Treatment: AGREED in part (10-Nov-2026: Rama's new-system input, confirmed in the read-back as an accurately recorded Phase 2 input); OPEN QUESTION in part (its design details, left for Phase 2). Not an approved V2 design decision. |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's statement about the legacy system: OPEN (his answer of 10-Nov-2026); no statement on the history, and none is inferred. (3) Rama's new-system input (10-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input): "Accept ChatGPT's suggestion." (his answer as written in the answers file ChatGPT prepared at his instruction, "Write all open & your suggestion accepted"). ChatGPT's suggestion, as put to him in batch 1: One central way of opening connections, so every connection gets an explicit, reviewed set of settings and mandatory safety settings cannot be left out. Connections for different purposes (for example read-only reports and write-capable runtime) may have different profiles, but each difference is explicit, centrally governed and checked. It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of D-04 and not a V2 design decision. Left for Phase 2 design, not decided: the connection profiles, which settings each profile holds, and how a missing setting is detected. Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). The history status is OPEN; the new-system input is confirmed as an accurately recorded Phase 2 input; its detailed design remains for Phase 2. |

### A03-03 — H-05 (STUDY question 8, the GTT part): "At most one ACTIVE GTT row per open trade"

|---|---|

| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | What was the requirement for the rule "at most one ACTIVE `gtt_state` row per open trade": enforcement by the database, enforcement by the code on every path that adds a GTT row, a non-normative comment (C1), or a description of expected normal behaviour without required enforcement (C2)? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies H-05 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 6.3). FACT: `core/schema.sql` L1205 to L1208 states the rule; no partial unique index or trigger enforces it; the adoption path checks before inserting; the recreate path closes the old ACTIVE row before placing the new GTT; the fresh-placement path (`orders/cnc_gtt.py` L209 to L221) does not check. Whether the fresh-placement path can run for a trade that already has an ACTIVE row depends on the GTT flows and is A11's to establish; Rama's OPEN answer does not settle it. |
| Impact | Not established. |
| Owner | Rama answers; Claude records. Topic: A11 (the GTT flows, and whether the fresh-placement path can create a second ACTIVE row); A03 (the DDL). |
| Raised with Rama | 10-Nov-2026 (batch 1, B1-Q3), after ChatGPT's review of the issue-discussion plan (fifth review, PASS); answered the same day; read back the same day (ChatGPT's review of the read-back: PASS); Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). |

Not an approved V2 design decision. |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's statement about the legacy system: OPEN (his answer of 10-Nov-2026); no statement on the history, and none is inferred. (3) Rama's new-system input (10-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input): "Accept ChatGPT's suggestion." (his answer as written in the answers file ChatGPT prepared at his instruction, "Write all open & your suggestion accepted"). ChatGPT's suggestion, as put to him in batch 1: Critical position and order rules are enforced at the strongest reliable layer: database constraints where the rule can be expressed and enforced safely, plus transactional checks in the code where needed; not every business rule is suited to a database constraint alone. It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of H-05 and not a V2 design decision. Left for Phase 2 design, not decided: which rules become database constraints and which stay transactional checks, and how each is verified. ### A03-04 — N-17 (STUDY question 11): A migration after a market-hours refusal

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-04 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | Was an automatic restart by the service manager after 15:30 meant to count as "the next off-market boot" at which a migration refused in market hours is applied? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies N-17 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 7.3). FACT: a market-hours start with a pending migration refuses and exits 1; the declared unit restarts it after 10 s. INTERPRETATION: every restart until 15:30 refuses again with a CRITICAL line and a new sentinel; the first start after 15:30 migrates and the boot continues. HISTORICAL: the 14-Jul migration rule (AC2, AC4) and the refusal text say the change applies at the next off-market boot; no source addresses an automatic restart. |
| Impact | Not established. |
| Owner | Rama answers; Claude records. Topic: A03 (the migration gate); A17 (the restart policy); A14 (the repeated sentinels); A01 (S-03, C4). |
| Raised with Rama | 10-Nov-2026 (batch 1, B1-Q4), after ChatGPT's review of the issue-discussion plan (fifth review, PASS); answered the same day; read back the same day (ChatGPT's review of the read-back: PASS); Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). |

Not an approved V2 design decision. |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's statement about the legacy system: OPEN (his answer of 10-Nov-2026); no statement on the history, and none is inferred. (3) Rama's new-system input (10-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input): "Accept ChatGPT's suggestion." (his answer as written in the answers file ChatGPT prepared at his instruction, "Write all open & your suggestion accepted"). ChatGPT's suggestion, as put to him in batch 1: A refused migration does not create an uncontrolled rapid retry and alert loop; it enters a visible blocked or pending state and resumes only under an explicitly authorised off-market migration procedure; the exact restart policy is for Phase 2. It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of N-17 and not a V2 design decision. Left for Phase 2 design, not decided: the restart policy, the blocked or pending state and its visibility, and the authorised off-market migration procedure. Rama confirmed the read-back on 10-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed, proceed"). As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: `set_trade_closure_axes` records how a trade was closed (two columns, `closure_source` and `exit_mechanism`). Its docstring (26-Jul-2026) says the whole write is guarded to rows whose `closure_source` is still empty. ### A03-30 — R-02 (STUDY question 15, part 1(b)): Trade status writes that do not check the current status

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-30 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | History: were these unchecked writes meant to differ from the checked ones? A: No; every status write was meant to check the current status, as the kill switch does. B: Yes; the final-state lock alone was meant to be enough. Other / qualify (per writer). OPEN. |
| Evidence | The AUDIT's findings: R-02 (ISSUE: a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way at each site). As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: six status writes on `trades` (for example the end-of-day CANCELLED write and the reconciler's FAILED write) change the status without checking what it is now. The database trigger blocks only changes away from a final status (CLOSED, CLOSED_MANUAL, FAILED, CANCELLED, REJECTED and its variants). So, if another thread moved the trade in the meantime (for example an entry filled just before the end-of-day write), an OPEN trade could be recorded as CANCELLED or FAILED. Whether such a race ever happened is not known. The sources (HISTORICAL; evidence only, not proof of what each writer was meant to do): a 07-Jul-2026 design gives a map of legal transitions but makes the trigger "deliberately" a final-state lock, "not a full transition matrix"; the same design calls the guarded store methods "the model to follow" and the unguarded raw writers "ILLEGAL-CAPABLE (the fix targets)". The kill switch (16-Jul-2026) checks the current status first, because an unconditional write "would clobber" what the fill thread had done. The AUDIT classifies these findings without Rama's answer (section 18); his answer is for the treatment round. |
| Impact | As recorded in each AUDIT record; not established beyond it. |
| Owner | Rama answers; Claude records. AUDIT Owner fields: R-02: A10, A11, A12 (the writers); A03 (the trigger's storage scope; part H owns its DDL). |

### A03-31 — R-03 (STUDY question 15, part 1(c)): Order CANCELLED writes that do not protect a filled order

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-31 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | History: were the end-of-day CANCELLED writes meant to be guarded in the same way? A: Yes, as the kill switch and the reconciler are. B: No. Other / qualify. OPEN. |
| Evidence | The AUDIT's findings: R-03 (ISSUE). As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: after cancelling at the broker, the end-of-day job (and a structure-exit path declared as not running) writes CANCELLED to the order without checking its current status; `orders` has no trigger. If a stop-loss or target leg filled in the gap and was already recorded COMPLETE, it would be overwritten as CANCELLED. The kill switch and the reconciler guard against exactly this ("must NEVER be overwritten with CANCELLED — above all COMPLETE"). Whether it ever happened is not known. The AUDIT classifies these findings without Rama's answer (section 18); his answer is for the treatment round. |

### A03-32 — P-07 (STUDY question 16(a)): Which trades count as "active"

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-32 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | History: what was the "active" set meant to cover? A: Every trade with live or possibly live exposure, including EXITING, PENDING and UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT (the docstring's "ALL"). B: Exactly PENDING_FILL, OPEN and PARTIAL (the docstring's list and the code). Other / qualify (per state). OPEN. |
| Evidence | The AUDIT's findings: P-07 (ISSUE: documentation against code). As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: the "active" set (PENDING_FILL, OPEN, PARTIAL) is used for the open-position cap, the same-symbol and opposite-position checks, the end-of-day exit gate, the allocator's capacity, the delivery position count and the sector figure. Its docstring says it counts "ALL" trades with live or about-to-be-live exposure, but lists only those three statuses. It leaves out EXITING, PENDING and UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT, which the code's own comments describe as possibly live at the broker (an UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT or crashed PENDING entry "may actually be LIVE") or as still holding capital (an EXITING trade stuck after a kill flatten). So such a trade does not count against the cap and does not block a new signal on the same symbol; whether the capital side covers it through its reservations is A09's. The AUDIT classifies these findings without Rama's answer (section 18); his answer is for the treatment round. |

Asked because the STUDY raises these history questions; the AUDIT does not need Rama's answers to classify them ("No; for treatment"). These records stay MATCH unless an answer conflicts with the intent they rely on; then they are re-examined under the AUDIT's proviso and shown to ChatGPT and Rama before any change. As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records, in three parts: (b) Executed trades: PENDING_FILL, OPEN, PARTIAL, EXITING, CLOSED, CLOSED_MANUAL. Read for the risk engine's daily trade and delivery counts, the signal processor's figures and its one-trade-per-symbol-and-direction rule. Its comment (FIX-181, 16-Jun-2026): failed, cancelled and rejected trades never use up the daily quota. Not counted: PENDING and UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT, besides FAILED, CANCELLED and REJECTED. (Two copies of this list elsewhere, one of them without PENDING_FILL, are A15's; handed off, not asked here.) (c) Settled trades: OPEN, PARTIAL, EXITING, CLOSED, CLOSED_MANUAL, the executed list without PENDING_FILL. Read by the risk engine and the signal processor's figures. Its comment (Bug B, 19-Jun-2026): a PENDING_FILL trade is counted once, through its live capital reservation, so that "settled + live_reservations partition today's quota usage with no overlap". (d) Open trades: OPEN and PARTIAL only. Read for the risk engine's open-position figure, the list of open trades (the fund manager's rebuild at start-up, the delivery GTT monitor, the reconciler and the end-of-day broker reconciliation), the end-of-day square-off's open positions and exit-leg cancels, the MIS exit-leg cancel, the target-retry candidates and the sector partition. Not included: EXITING, PENDING_FILL, PENDING and UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT. The AUDIT records no separate finding on this list; three MATCH records (P-16, P-24, P-36) rely on stated intent that names OPEN and PARTIAL. ### A03-34 — P-06, R-09, P-36 (STUDY question 16(e)): Which order statuses count as finished, and which as still open

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A03-34 |
| Topic | A03 |
| Question or discrepancy | History: were these different lists meant to differ? A: No; all were meant to follow the state machine's one list. B: Yes, on purpose (say where). Other / qualify (per list). OPEN. |
| Evidence | The AUDIT's findings: P-06, R-09 (ISSUE); P-36 (MATCH: the stop-loss lookup uses the state machine's final statuses). As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: the order state machine names four final statuses (COMPLETE, CANCELLED, FAILED, EXPIRED). The store's two cancel reads (end-of-day, MIS square-off) leave out EXPIRED, so an expired leg is treated as still resting and gets a cancel attempt; they list REJECTED, which is never stored. The start-up reload of open orders leaves out UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT and one spelling of TRIGGER PENDING. Raw SQL elsewhere copies its own lists, which differ from site to site (R-09; it also includes one copied list of final trade statuses, without REJECTED). The state machine's own rule: "No string literals duplicated". The AUDIT classifies these findings without Rama's answer (section 18); his answer is for the treatment round. |

It is a write guard, not one of the five read sets of question 16. As put to Rama in batch 3, from the AUDIT records: the in-flight recovery treats three statuses as recovery states (UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT, PENDING, PENDING_FILL), and two of its three store setters accept all three; `fail_recovery_trade` marks FAILED only the first two. For a PENDING_FILL trade it does nothing and the caller logs "already resolved", which is not quite accurate. No source says why PENDING_FILL is left out. (3) Rama's new-system input (11-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input): ACCEPT (his answer, recorded in the answers file ChatGPT prepared at his instruction, "My side reply for all questions: OPEN & YOUR SUGGESTION ARE ACCEPTED"). ChatGPT's worksheet suggestion, as accepted: ACCEPT the separation between scheduled runs and manual operator runs. A manual run should not be silently skipped. If any run is skipped, it must clearly report and record why. Rama's summary: "the separation between scheduled and manual maintenance runs. Manual operator runs must not be silently skipped; any skip must state and record its reason." Rama's additional input (a Phase 2 input, system-wide, not limited to database jobs): the new system should provide a comparable end-of-day operational execution report wherever technically possible and appropriate, covering relevant scheduled tasks, tools, scripts, files or processes and system components (and, where useful and suitable, important functions or operational steps), making clear what ran and what did not. Minimum statuses: COMPLETED / SUCCESS; FAILED, with a concise reason; MISSED / not executed when expected; SKIPPED BY DESIGN, with the reason; NO SIGNAL / UNKNOWN / UNTRACKED where execution cannot be established. Useful fields, where available: scheduled and actual start and completion times; task, job, script, tool or component identity; status and concise failure or skip reason; the detection or completion-signal source (for example heartbeat or exit-code file); duration and exit code; relevant roster or configuration changes; monitoring and alert-path health and report integrity. A consolidated end-of-day view with task-level detail; never SUCCESS without a valid completion signal. Not a request to instrument every low-level function or to produce noisy logs: instrumentation at meaningful operational boundaries. For this entry: a manual run returns a clear outcome and reason, and the end-of-day report covers the database maintenance job like other suitable jobs. Reference: the legacy end-of-day Cron Officer report of 01-Oct-2026 (HISTORICAL; cited by type and date only). Carried to A16 and A14 as an agreed Phase 2 input, not raised again. The accepted wording is the suggestion in ChatGPT's worksheet, which Rama saw when he answered; Claude's batch 4 proposal is the reference and crosswalk only, and its extra words are not recorded as accepted (ChatGPT's review of the read-back). It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of any finding and not a V2 design decision. | Evidence | The AUDIT classifies AU-04 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 6.3). FACT: `scripts/zerodha_login.py` `save_token` writes `expires_at` as the next 05:00 IST; its only non-test reader is the health payload, which copies it; `is_token_valid` and the start gate use the IST calendar date only; the declared 05:00 token clean-up (`config/cron_registry.yaml`, a declaration, not proof of a run) does not read the field. HISTORICAL: decision E (2026-04-17) names the field without a use; the documented expiry times differ (IC-22: 05:00 and 06:00 IST). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the broker's real token lifetime in the legacy period (no repository evidence). |

Not an approved V2 design decision. |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL as in Evidence, unchanged. (2) Rama's statement about the legacy system: OPEN (his answer of 13-Nov-2026); no statement on the history of `expires_at`, and none is inferred. His operational background of 13-Nov-2026, given before his answers and recorded as his statement about how the system was run (not as an answer to this question): the system created the token headlessly at about 08:15 IST on each trading day (it does not start on weekends or holidays) and traded until the end of the day; a cron job deleted the token file at 05:00 IST the next morning, and the cycle repeated; once the session was over the token was not needed, except for manual analytics or personal API calls; he understood the token to be valid until 07:00 AM the next day and to be ended earlier only by a newer token created the same day. External context (Zerodha's pages, checked on 13-Nov-2026; current pages, not evidence of the broker's behaviour in the legacy period): the Kite Connect API documentation (`https://www.kite.trade/docs/connect/v3/user/`) states that the access token expires at 6 AM on the next day unless invalidated earlier through the API logout or by a master logout from the Kite Web terminal; Zerodha's Kite Connect API FAQs state that tokens become invalid if a new session is generated using the same API key; Kite Connect forum replies describe an operational flush at about 07:00 to 07:35 AM (forum statements, not the documented contract). The 05:00 cron deletion is a local file action, not the broker's expiry. (3) Rama's new-system input (13-Nov-2026; a Phase 2 input): he asked ChatGPT for a suitable suggestion based on his background, and then accepted ChatGPT's refined suggestion ("Accept ChatGPT's refined suggestion", in the Claude chat): use one explicit token lifecycle contract, keeping two concepts separate. Broker token validity: take the broker's actual expiry from the authoritative Zerodha contract (currently documented as 06:00 AM next day); treat invalidation by a newer session, a master logout or an explicit API invalidation as separate events; do not use the local file-clean-up time as a substitute for broker validity. Local token-file retention and clean-up: treat the 05:00 AM cron as a local retention and deletion schedule, not proof of broker expiry; if the token is needed for permitted post-session analytics or manual API use, the retention rule must account for it; the local deletion time and the broker's expiry must not share one ambiguous `expires_at` field. Token creation and reuse: one controlled daily token creation after the broker's daily reset window and before the trading system starts; reuse that token during its valid session; avoid needless same-day token generation, because a newer session can invalidate the previous one; control the manual, incident and concurrent creation paths so that no process unexpectedly replaces the live process's token. Source and verification: use Zerodha's official API documentation as the primary contract; verify the operational reset time with Zerodha before any time-critical implementation; never assume validity beyond the documented expiry. It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of AU-04 and not a V2 design decision. Left for Phase 2 design, not decided: the contract's fields, the retention period and the creation schedule. Rama confirmed the read-back on 13-Nov-2026 ("Confirmed as written"). The history status is OPEN; the new-system input is confirmed as an accurately recorded Phase 2 input; its detailed design remains for Phase 2. |

### A04-16 — OA-08 (STUDY question 11): A placement that timed out: "failed" or "unknown until checked"

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A04-16 |
| Topic | A04 |
| Question or discrepancy | When a placement timed out, so that it was not known whether the broker had received the order, was the order's own record meant to say "failed", or "unknown until checked with the broker"? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies OA-08 ISSUE (section 8.3: the sources conflict on whether a placement timeout is a failure; the code follows the earliest). FACT: the adapter's `place_order` marks the internal order FAILED on every exception from the SDK call, a timeout and a 429 included; FAILED is final; the order state machine's UNKNOWN_IN_FLIGHT state, added by FIX-068 (16-May-2026) for a timeout on placement, is entered by no code. HISTORICAL: ZA7 and ZA8 (17-Apr-2026): FAILED on any exception; the placer's FIX-068 comment (16-May-2026): "we don't know if the order reached the broker. Do NOT mark FAILED". KNOWN DEFECT rule (the AUDIT's section 1.6, the A01 AUDIT's four conditions): conditions (a) to (c) hold; (d) fails, because ZA7 and ZA8 intend exactly this outcome. |
| Impact | None established in code beyond the in-memory record of an id that nothing reads; the trade-level consequence is A10's. OA-08 stays ISSUE. |
| Owner | Rama answers; Claude records. Topic: A04 (the adapter's transition); A10 (the trade status); A12 (the recovery). |
| Raised with Rama | 13-Nov-2026 (batch 3, B3-Q1), after ChatGPT's final review of the revised batch 3 proposal (PASS); answered the same day in the ChatGPT chat, after reading ChatGPT's plain-English explanation of the batch; read back the same day (ChatGPT's review of the read-back: PASS, with the boundary that the confirmation covers the full suggestion wording); Rama confirmed the read-back on 13-Nov-2026 ("Confirmed as written"). |

Not an approved V2 design decision, treatment, architecture or implementation. |
| Resolution | (1) FACT / HISTORICAL as in Evidence, unchanged. External context (Zerodha's Kite Connect documentation, checked on 13-Nov-2026; current pages, not evidence of the broker's behaviour in the legacy period): a placement through the API "does not imply its successful execution"; the documentation recommends checking the order's history or fetching it by its order id, and offers a `tag` to identify an order; the exceptions page names NetworkException and HTTP 504 and does not say whether a request that failed in that way was received. (2) Rama's statement about the legacy system: OPEN (his answer of 13-Nov-2026); no statement on the history, and none is inferred. (4) "A broker tag can help correlate an order, but must not be represented as an idempotency guarantee.". He read ChatGPT's shorter plain-English summary of it before answering; his confirmation of the read-back covers this full wording. It is not an answer to the history question, not a reclassification of OA-08 and not a V2 design decision. ### A04-17 — OA-16, OA-15 related (STUDY question 12): The protected-MARKET band: MIS auto-square-off only, or every MARKET order

|---|---|

UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the broker rule in the legacy period, and whether production ran `8f9afe0`. |

| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies OA-20 ISSUE, with a MATCH part (section 8.3: the result cannot carry the kind of the failure). FACT: `cancel_order` and `modify_order` return `success=False` with the exception's text for a broker refusal (as designed, the MATCH part) and equally for a timeout, a lost connection, a rejected token or a broker 429; the result carries no class, no code and no "sent or not" mark. ### A04-22 — MO-16 (STUDY question 22): The orders the stuck-PARTIAL timeout was meant for

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A04-22 |
| Topic | A04 |
| Question or discrepancy | Which orders was the stuck-PARTIAL timeout meant for: the exit legs SL, TGT and EOD, or the ENTRY legs? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies MO-16 ISSUE (section 12.3: documentation against code with conflicting same-day sources, IC-15; the docstring is contradicted by the code's test). FACT: the stuck-PARTIAL timeout (5 minutes) applies to every leg except SL, TGT and EOD; the code writes and tracks only the legs ENTRY, SL, TGT and EOD (the schema also allows CO and CANCEL, which no code writes as a leg), so among those legs the test selects ENTRY; an ENTRY is cancelled at its first PARTIAL, so the timeout reaches an ENTRY only if that cancel failed; an exit leg stuck in PARTIAL is never cancelled by the monitor. HISTORICAL (30-May-2026): the FIX-130 docstring ("Exit legs (SL/TGT/EOD) keep the timeout path") and a comment of the same change ("non-ENTRY legs") against the FIX-128 commit ("cancel ENTRY stuck in PARTIAL > 5 min") and the configuration comment ("entry orders"). |
| Impact | Not established (no PARTIAL observed live, HISTORICAL). MO-16 stays ISSUE. |

### A04-25 — MO-13 (STUDY question 25): A cancel that failed because the broker call timed out

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Issue ID | A04-25 |
| Topic | A04 |
| Question or discrepancy | When a cancel failed because the broker call timed out (not because the broker refused it), was the order meant to be marked FAILED and dropped from polling like a refused cancel? |
| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies MO-13 ISSUE, with a MATCH part (section 12.3: the six paths handle a failed cancel differently, and the PARTIAL paths drop a partly filled order without FAILED or the partial-fill report). FACT: on the fill-timeout path any cancel failure, a timeout included, marks the order FAILED, stops polling it and raises the orphan alert, as OM7 (17-Apr-2026) states ("NEVER leave broker order open silently"; the MATCH part). INTERPRETATION: a timed-out cancel may still have taken effect at the broker. See also A04-18 (the adapter's side). |
| Impact | Not established; occurrence UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. MO-13 stays ISSUE; no count changes. |

### A04-26 — OA-22 (STUDY question 34): Trailing a cover order's stop through the smart target manager in Live

|---|---|

| Evidence | The AUDIT classifies OA-22 ISSUE, latent (section 8.3). FACT: `modify_order` always sends the variety "regular", so it cannot modify a cover order (variety "co"); under `main.py`'s wiring no new entry takes the cover-order protocol. HISTORICAL: the design P8_P13 (17-Apr-2026) trails a cover order's stop by modifying its trigger price; a code comment calls the cover-order path dormant.
